> **Last controlled update:** 2026-07-16 (Phase 5 — created)

# 11thONUS Requirements Traceability & Implementation Matrix

**This is a permanent platform governance document, not a one-time phase deliverable.** It is the single place where every approved requirement, rule and principle in the 11thONUS documentation suite can be traced from its source through its planned technical implementation to its future test coverage. It is maintained for the lifetime of the product — see the companion [Traceability Maintenance Guide](traceability-maintenance-guide.md) for how it stays current.

## Purpose and Strategy

**Why this matrix exists.** TRD Chapter 23 §23.4 defines the required traceability chain: *Platform Constitution Principle → PRD Requirement → TRD Requirement → Implementation → Test.* Until Phase 4, that chain could not be built reliably because three sets of requirement IDs collided (`FR-RP`, `OP`) and one set didn't exist yet (PRD4's unnumbered functional requirements). Phase 4 made every ID globally unique. Phase 5 uses that stability to build the chain itself, for all 934 requirement/rule/principle identifiers currently declared in the authoritative documentation suite.

**Why each column exists:**
- **Requirement ID / Type / Title / Source Document / Section** — the *what and where*: makes every requirement locatable in one lookup, and machine-parseable by ID (never by page number, which changes).
- **Related Decision IDs** — links each requirement to any governance decision that shapes or constrains it (from the Decision Register), so nobody implements against a requirement whose behavior is still under an open decision without seeing that flag.
- **Related Constitutional Principle** — intended to anchor each requirement to the constitutional value it serves. **Honest limitation, disclosed rather than fabricated:** no PRD or TRD document in the current suite cites a specific `CP-XXX` principle next to an individual requirement (verified by repository-wide search before this matrix was built). Populating this column with invented linkages would misrepresent the source material, so it is intentionally left `—` suite-wide and flagged here as a known gap for a future, deliberate principle-mapping pass — not a Phase 5 oversight.
- **Domain** — the owning business domain (of the 15 defined in TRD23 §23.7 / Canonical Reference §5), so implementation work can be grouped and staffed by domain.
- **Planned Technical Module / Firestore Collections / Cloud Functions / Frontend Screens / API** — the *engineering-support* columns: they tell a future engineer or AI coding agent which Domain Service (TRD11), which Firestore collections (TRD10 §10.4 Collection Ownership Matrix — cited, not invented), and which class of Cloud Function/screen/API surface a requirement belongs to. These are planning-level (domain/module granularity), never specific function or screen names, because no such design exists yet — inventing them here would be implementation, which this phase is explicitly forbidden from doing.
- **Acceptance Criteria / Future Test Reference** — placeholders by design (per the task brief): they will be populated once the Engineering Standards (Phase 6) define testable criteria and once actual tests exist. Leaving them as an honest "Not yet defined" is more useful to a future engineer than a fabricated criterion.
- **Implementation Status** — defaults to `Not Started` for all 934 rows, since no application code exists yet (Constitution Preamble; every phase report to date).
- **Dependencies** — any *still-open* (not yet CONFIRMED) Decision Register item that blocks this requirement, so an engineer or agent sees the blocker without cross-referencing the register manually.
- **Notes** — an explicit stop-sign for any requirement affected by an open decision, echoing TRD22 §22.40's stop-and-report principle directly inside the matrix.

**How this supports engineering.** A future engineer can filter by Domain or Planned Technical Module to scope a work package, see the exact PRD/TRD source and section to read before writing code, and see immediately whether any open decision blocks that work — all from one document, without re-deriving domain ownership or hunting for the source paragraph.

**How this supports testing.** Every requirement has a stable, citable ID and a `Future Test Reference` placeholder ready to be filled in Phase 6/7 with the actual test identifier — the matrix becomes the index that proves test coverage exists for every requirement, not just for the features someone remembered to test.

**How this supports future maintenance.** IDs are permanent (Phase 4's rule: never reused, never silently dropped). When a requirement changes, its row is updated in place; when it's deprecated, it's marked, not deleted (mirroring TRD23 §23.28 and the ID Mapping's historical-integrity rule). The [Maintenance Guide](traceability-maintenance-guide.md) defines the exact procedure.

**How this supports future AI coding agents.** Every row is a flat, individually addressable record keyed by a globally unique ID — an agent can look up a single requirement's full context (source, domain, planned module, open blockers) in one lookup instead of re-reading entire PRD/TRD chapters, and the `Dependencies`/`Notes` columns give it a direct, explicit stop-and-report signal (TRD22 §22.40) before it ever touches decision-gated behavior.

**Rule for coding agents (repeating the suite-wide rule for clarity in this document specifically):** `Implementation Status = Not Started` on every row means no code exists. An agent must not treat a row's presence in this matrix, nor its `Planned Technical Module`/`Planned Firestore Collections` values, as authorization to implement — those are planning fields, not build instructions. Implementation begins only after Version 1.0 documentation freeze, per the Constitution Preamble and every phase report to date.

## Scope and Method

**In scope:** every requirement, business rule, product decision, principle and rule-table entry declared in the Platform Constitution, all 11 PRD files, all 17 TRD chapter files, and the Commerce Knowledge Standard — 934 identifiers total, extracted programmatically (not by hand) to guarantee completeness, then spot-verified against the Requirements ID Audit's and Phase 4's published counts.

**Out of scope (by design, not oversight):** the Decision Register itself (its 103 DEC-* records are decisions *about* requirements, not requirements — they are linked via the `Related Decision IDs` column, not duplicated as rows); the Assumptions Register's `AS-*` rows (already a controlled restatement of TRD23's own `AS-*` assumptions, which *are* included as rows here); Knowledge Studio and Rules Studio (narrative/process documents with no declared IDs of their own, confirmed by the same extraction pass finding zero matches); archived/superseded documents and audit evidence (historical, never re-traced, per the standing Phase 2 rule).

## How to Use This Matrix

Search by Requirement ID (each is a unique, `code`-formatted token). Each entry below is grouped by its source document, in suite order (Constitution → PRD Stage 1/Sections 1–10 → TRD Chapters 1–23 → Commerce Knowledge Standard), matching the [docs index](../README.md)'s document order. Within each group, rows are sorted by ID.

## Validation Statement

This matrix was validated after generation (full method and results in the [Phase 5 Implementation Report](../05-implementation/reports/phase-5-traceability-matrix-report-2026-07-16.md)): 100% of the 934 extracted identifiers are present as exactly one row each; 0 duplicate Requirement IDs; 0 orphan requirements (every row's Source Document and Section are populated from the actual declaring location); every `Related Decision IDs` reference was verified to exist in the current Decision Register; every requirement has exactly one authoritative source (no ID is declared in more than one current, non-archived document); `Implementation Status` defaults to `Not Started` on all 934 rows; no requirement wording was changed to build this matrix — titles are extracted verbatim (truncated for table width where noted) from the source documents, never rewritten.
